      21 +// First-of-month tick timestamps spanning [minMs, maxMs], thin
         +ned to ~<=12                                                   
      22 +// ticks. A numeric time XAxis won't auto-generate readable dat
         +e ticks, so we                                                 
      23 +// supply them explicitly (fixes a bare x-axis in the scatter m
         +ode).                                                          
      24 +export function monthTicks(minMs, maxMs) {                     
      25 +  if (!(maxMs >= minMs)) return [];                            
      26 +  const lo = new Date(minMs);                                  
      27 +  let y = lo.getUTCFullYear();                                 
      28 +  let m = lo.getUTCMonth();                                    
      29 +  const hi = new Date(maxMs);                                  
      30 +  const span = (hi.getUTCFullYear() - y) * 12 + (hi.getUTCMonth
         +() - m);                                                       
      31 +  const step = Math.max(1, Math.ceil((span + 1) / 12));        
      32 +  const ticks = [];                                            
      33 +  let t = Date.UTC(y, m, 1);                                   
      34 +  while (t <= maxMs) {                                         
      35 +    ticks.push(t);                                             
      36 +    m += step;                                                 
      37 +    while (m >= 12) { m -= 12; y += 1; }                       
      38 +    t = Date.UTC(y, m, 1);                                     
      39 +  }                                                            
      40 +  return ticks;                                                
      41 +}                                                              
      42 +                                                               
      43 +function fmtMonth(t) {                                         
      44 +  return new Date(t).toLocaleDateString(undefined,             
      45 +    { year: "2-digit", month: "short" });                      
      46 +}                                                              
      47 +                                                               
      48  function fmtScore(s) {
      49    return s > 0 ? `+${s}` : `${s}`;
      50  }

● Fixed and committed (43 frontend tests pass, build clean).

  What was wrong

  In the "Individual items" (scatter) mode, the x-axis used a numeric time axis
  (type="number" + scale="time" with a dataMin/dataMax domain). Recharts doesn't
  reliably auto-generate readable date ticks in that configuration — so the
  axis rendered with no proper month labels. (The "Monthly average" mode uses a
  categorical month axis and was unaffected.)

  The fix

  I now compute the ticks explicitly: a monthTicks(min, max) helper generates
  first-of-month timestamps spanning the data range, thinned to ~12 max so a
  multi-year window stays readable, and the axis uses an explicit [xMin, xMax]
  domain with a MMM 'YY formatter. Single-point ranges are padded so a tick
  still renders.

  I added unit tests for monthTicks (month-aligned ticks across a short range,
  thinning over multi-year, empty/invalid range) — that's the part I can verify
  headlessly.

  To see it

  Frontend-only → no restart needed; Vite hot-reloads. Refresh the run-view,
  switch to "Individual items," and the x-axis should now show month/year labels
  along the bottom.

  Honest note, same as before: jsdom can't render the actual SVG, so I verified
  the tick logic in tests and confirmed the build compiles, but I couldn't
  machine-verify the rendered axis. Give it a quick look — if the labels are
  spaced oddly or the format isn't what you want (e.g. you'd prefer full month
  names, or quarterly ticks), tell me and I'll adjust the density/format.

❯ 1. Let's remove the monthly average option and the individual items radio

● Both make sense — the scatter view is the interesting one, and per-category
  filtering lets you isolate (say) Reddit sentiment. Two things follow:

  - The graph becomes always the scatter + average line (no mode toggle).
  - The average line must be recomputed on the frontend from the selected
  categories' items (the server's sentiment_timeline is over all categories, so
  it can't reflect a filtered subset). The frontend already has every item, so
  this is straightforward.

  Let me rewrite the component. First, RunView passes all items (tagged with
  category) and drops the now-unused props.

● Both changes are in and committed (44 frontend + 174 backend green, build
  clean).

  What changed

  1. Removed the mode toggle. The graph is now always the per-item scatter with 
  the average line overlaid — no radio buttons. That's the interesting view, as
  you said.

  2. Per-category checkboxes above the graph. One checkbox per category that has
  dots on the graph, all on by default. Toggling any of them re-scopes both the
  dots and the average line to just the selected categories — computed
  client-side from the item data. So you can uncheck News to see Reddit/forums
  sentiment on its own, instead of it being flattened by high-volume positive
  news. The headline "Overall sentiment" and the undated/unknown notes also
  update with the selection.

  Why client-side: the server's monthly averages are over all categories, so a
  filtered view has to be re-aggregated in the browser — which is fine since
  every item is already in the payload. I kept the x-axis month-tick fix from
  before, so the time axis stays labelled.

  Details: the checkbox row only appears when there's more than one graphable
  category; deselecting everything shows a "Select a category…" prompt; dots
  stay clickable and the tooltip still shows category/source/date/score/summary.
  I also removed the now-dead mode-toggle CSS.

  To see it

  Frontend-only → no restart; Vite hot-reloads. Refresh the run-view: you'll see
  the category checkboxes above the scatter, and unchecking one will redraw the
  dots and the trend line for the remaining categories.

  Same honest caveat as before: I can verify the filtering/aggregation logic and
  the headline math in jsdom (and did — e.g. isolating Reddit flips the average
  to −1), but not the actual rendered SVG. Give the interaction a quick look;
  if the line doesn't visibly update when you toggle a category, or the checkbox
  set isn't what you expect, tell me and I'll adjust.
  ⎿  Stop says: ✦ 30 memories woven into the palace — plan, items, sentiment

❯ I see that a lot of reddit results are 'undated'. How come?                   

● Let me check empirically — look at what Tavily actually returns for Reddit
  results (dates), and whether Reddit URLs even contain an extractable date.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Let me check the actual data — pull the Reddit-domain items from recent runs
  and their dates.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Good catch — I dug into the actual data. Your latest run's reddit category has
  34 items; 16 are dated, 18 (~53%) undated.

  Here's the "how come":

  1. We only get publish dates from Tavily's published_date field, and for
  Reddit posts Tavily returns it inconsistently — it has a date for about half
  the Reddit results and simply omits it for the rest. (Reddit isn't a
  conventional news publisher, so Tavily's date coverage for reddit.com is
  patchy compared to news sites, which are almost always dated.)
  2. Our URL-date fallback can't help here. For news sites the date is often in
  the path (/2026/02/18/…), but Reddit permalinks look like
  reddit.com/r/apple/comments/1vhk9hd/title_slug/ — the 1vhk9hd is a base-36
  post ID, not a decodable date. So there's nothing in the URL to fall back on.

  So: an undated Reddit item = "Tavily gave us the post but no date, and the URL
  has no date to extract." It's still scored and shown in the list — it just
  can't be placed on the time axis (and, now that you can filter, it won't
  appear as a dot when you isolate Reddit).

  If you want Reddit items reliably dated

  The clean way is Reddit's own JSON: appending .json to a post permalink
  returns created_utc (the exact post time), no auth needed. I could backfill
  dates for undated reddit.com items that way. Honest trade-offs:

  - Extra HTTP calls — one small fetch per undated Reddit item (bounded, but
  it's outside the Tavily seam).
  - Reddit rate-limits / blocks bots — unauthenticated requests need a
  descriptive User-Agent and are throttled (~60/min), and Reddit increasingly
  blocks datacenter IPs, so it can fail for some items. I'd make it best-effort
  (item stays undated if the fetch fails) so it never breaks a run.
  - It's Reddit-specific plumbing — the same problem exists for other
  social/forum sources (X, forums), which have no general solution.

  Want me to add the best-effort Reddit-JSON date backfill? Or is it acceptable
  that social/forum content is partly undated (scored and listed, just not on
  the timeline)? I'd lean toward leaving it unless dated Reddit coverage matters
  to you — but it's a quick, self-contained add if you want it.

✻ Brewed for 1m 27s
